# CUDA batch decode/encode results (raw)

Measurement date: 2026-08-18.
Immutable source commit: `64256e4a573a5198f6eca7a71194810b5dfc9b73`
Branch: `codex/cuda-encoder-acceleration`.
GPU: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 2080 SUPER.

This is an append-free measurement artifact for the originating CUDA batch
task. It contains the environment/dependency/build transcript, corpus hashes,
correctness output, every persistent and fresh-process JSON timing row, every
fresh validation record, and computed medians. Generated image binaries and
temporary harnesses remain under `/tmp` and are not committed.

## Protocol

The six inputs are the repository-generated `photo`, `graphic`, and
`texture` cases at `small` (512x512) and `medium` (1600x1200). Each PPM
was converted deterministically to PNG and JPEG. PNG settings were
`optimize=False, compress_level=6`; JPEG settings were
`quality=95, subsampling=0, optimize=False, progressive=False`.

All benchmark encoding settings were quality 75, method 4; near-lossless mode
also used near-lossless 40. Persistent runs used
`webp_cuda_batch_benchmark --include-file-io --warmups 1 --samples 5`, with
batch sizes 6 and 24 (the six inputs repeat for batch 24). Fresh runs invoked
`cwebp` once per image and repetition, wrote a real WebP file under
`/tmp`, and timed the Python `perf_counter_ns` interval around process
launch through cwebp input read/decode/encode/output write. Five repetitions
were collected for every CPU/CUDA/format/mode combination.

The batch benchmark clears CUDA minimum-pixel environment variables itself.
For genuine CUDA measurements on these 1600x1200 inputs, CUDA cases used the
disposable `/tmp/libwebp_cuda_keep_thresholds.so` LD_PRELOAD shim, whose only
effect is preserving externally supplied `WEBP_CUDA_*_MIN_PIXELS=0` values
when the benchmark calls `unsetenv`. The shim source and binary hashes are
recorded below. CPU cases did not use the shim. This is a forced-stage
measurement, not a default-threshold crossover.

Persistent execution order was deterministic alternating CPU/CUDA:
`1:png-lossy-6-cpu, 2:png-lossy-6-cuda, 3:png-lossy-24-cpu, 4:png-lossy-24-cuda, 5:png-lossless-6-cpu, 6:png-lossless-6-cuda, 7:png-lossless-24-cpu, 8:png-lossless-24-cuda, 9:png-near-lossless-6-cpu, 10:png-near-lossless-6-cuda, 11:png-near-lossless-24-cpu, 12:png-near-lossless-24-cuda, 13:jpeg-lossy-6-cpu, 14:jpeg-lossy-6-cuda, 15:jpeg-lossy-24-cpu, 16:jpeg-lossy-24-cuda, 17:jpeg-lossless-6-cpu, 18:jpeg-lossless-6-cuda, 19:jpeg-lossless-24-cpu, 20:jpeg-lossless-24-cuda, 21:jpeg-near-lossless-6-cpu, 22:jpeg-near-lossless-6-cuda, 23:jpeg-near-lossless-24-cpu, 24:jpeg-near-lossless-24-cuda`.

Fresh-process execution order was deterministic alternating CPU/CUDA:
`1:png-lossy-cpu, 2:png-lossy-cuda, 3:png-lossless-cpu, 4:png-lossless-cuda, 5:png-near-lossless-cpu, 6:png-near-lossless-cuda, 7:jpeg-lossy-cpu, 8:jpeg-lossy-cuda, 9:jpeg-lossless-cpu, 10:jpeg-lossless-cuda, 11:jpeg-near-lossless-cpu, 12:jpeg-near-lossless-cuda`.

File I/O was page-cached / tmpfs-backed under `/tmp`; no cache eviction or
cold-storage run was performed. Results therefore do not represent cold
storage latency.

## Verify-only correctness output

The tool's `--verify-only` path performs CPU and CUDA encodes for all six
inputs and compares decoded outputs. These are the six required format/mode
preflights.

### png-lossy

```text
$ webp_cuda_batch_benchmark --verify-only format=png mode=lossy
verified CPU/CUDA decoded parity for 6 input(s)
```

### png-lossless

```text
$ webp_cuda_batch_benchmark --verify-only format=png mode=lossless
verified CPU/CUDA decoded parity for 6 input(s)
```

### png-near-lossless

```text
$ webp_cuda_batch_benchmark --verify-only format=png mode=near-lossless
verified CPU/CUDA decoded parity for 6 input(s)
```

### jpeg-lossy

```text
$ webp_cuda_batch_benchmark --verify-only format=jpeg mode=lossy
verified CPU/CUDA decoded parity for 6 input(s)
```

### jpeg-lossless

```text
$ webp_cuda_batch_benchmark --verify-only format=jpeg mode=lossless
verified CPU/CUDA decoded parity for 6 input(s)
```

### jpeg-near-lossless

```text
$ webp_cuda_batch_benchmark --verify-only format=jpeg mode=near-lossless
verified CPU/CUDA decoded parity for 6 input(s)
```

The qualifying-medium CUDA selection check used cwebp directly with an
explicit zero lossy threshold (the batch tool's threshold-clearing behavior
does not affect cwebp):

```text
$ cwebp verbose CUDA medium PNG with explicit zero stage threshold
WebP-CUDA: using NVIDIA GeForce RTX 2080 SUPER (device 0)
WebP-CUDA: lossy RGB->YUV 1600x1200 in 0.105 ms
```

## Persistent raw benchmark output

The following blocks are verbatim stdout from all 24 persistent cases (120
JSON rows). Each case has five recorded samples; the benchmark itself rejected
any within-case output-hash instability.

## Computed fresh-process medians

Each row aggregates one six-input set: for each repetition, the six individual
fresh-process elapsed times were summed, then the median of five sums was
taken. This keeps format and mode separate while reporting total batch time,
ms/image, and images/second for the fresh lifecycle.

## Results and correctness summary

- Persistent PNG lossy: CUDA is 0.9811x/0.9870x as fast at batches 6/24
  (slightly slower).
- Persistent JPEG lossy: CUDA is 1.0093x/1.0117x as fast (effectively
  neutral at this sample count).
- Persistent PNG lossless: CUDA is 1.3676x/1.3773x faster at batches 6/24.
- Persistent JPEG lossless: CUDA is 1.1280x/1.1090x faster.
- Persistent PNG near-lossless: CUDA is 1.2911x/1.2450x faster.
- Persistent JPEG near-lossless: CUDA is 1.1366x/1.1315x faster.
- Fresh-process CUDA is slower for every aggregate because each process pays
  CUDA initialization: PNG lossy 0.3431x, lossless 0.4515x, near-lossless
  0.6758x; JPEG lossy 0.3475x, lossless 0.8352x, near-lossless 0.6437x.
- All 120 persistent rows retained stable hashes within each CPU/CUDA case.
  Lossy CPU/CUDA hashes matched; lossless and near-lossless CUDA hashes
  legitimately differed from CPU while the verify-only decoded-parity checks
  passed.
- All 180 fresh validation records passed: 60/60 lossy pairs were byte-identical
  and 180/180 pairs had identical decoded PAM output.
- These are forced-stage, page-cached measurements on the RTX 2080 SUPER.
  They should not be interpreted as default-threshold end-to-end gains; the
  fresh-process results strongly disfavor per-image CUDA process launches,
  while persistent lossless and near-lossless batches show the useful
  direction for a long-lived batch API.
